### Thinking

So the decision logic hinges on three conditions: `held_ok` requires all three gates V1, V2, and V3 to pass, but since V2 failed with a probability of 0.16, that rules out the TYPE-WRITTEN classification. The system then checks if the training lift condition holds (V4_train AND V4_train_vs_der), and if not, it defaults to CONTEXT-ONLY, with HOST-DAMAGED and NO-WRITE taking precedence if their conditions are met.

The key insight is that V1 shows a genuine held-transfer effect—the wire lifts held predictions by 1.337 nats, significantly beating the shuffled-label null (p=0.0005)—but V2 reveals this lift isn't class-specific. The own-class drop versus anti-class drop on held predictions doesn't exceed what the paired-permutation null would predict (p=0.16), meaning the held effect is generic rather than reflecting true membership abstraction transfer.

V3 passing (true wire beats deranged wire on held predictions) confirms the effect is content-dependent, not a wiring artifact—the deranged wire actively anti-licenses at -0.955 nats, so the true wire's +1.337 dominates. But this content-dependence doesn't rescue V2's failure: the held lift simply isn't cleanly own-versus-anti class-specific. Since held_ok requires all three gates to pass, the verdict stands as MEMORIZED-ONLY.

The train-predicate contrast is stark: the wire achieves 8.833 nats versus the deranged baseline of -8.634, a massive binding effect on trained predicates. On held predicates, the transfer is much weaker (1.337 nats, still beating nulls) and fails the class-specificity test—so the membership abstraction doesn't cleanly install as a weight edge. This is pure memorization: the weights capture the surface associations they were trained on, with minimal generalization to the held set.

The §14 audit required a fair coverage test, and this gradient-touched bare-NP frame experiment is exactly that. Under fair coverage conditions, the verdict holds as memorized-only—the coverage gap didn't overturn the tape-residency finding. The s317 deliver leg, demoted to one-sided in s322, is now being re-tested fairly under these conditions.

The nuance here is that V1 and V3 both passed, so there's a non-trivial held transfer that beats the nulls—it's not zero generalization. But it fails class-specificity at V2, which is the key qualification. The memorized-only verdict sits at a boundary: the held lift exists and transfers, but doesn't generalize across classes.

This aligns with the modal prediction of 35% for memorized-only, so the calibration checks out. The consequences are clear: weights bind predicate associations, not the abstraction itself, and tape-residency of judgments holds under fair coverage. The honest re-test still lands negative on the abstraction transfer, even though the weight-side testing is now fairly done. Now I'm executing the batch workflow: committing the type-write v2 results autonomously, updating the injectable relations documentation, index

```
train_lift = V4t ∧ V4tvd →  both pass → True
∴ ¬held_ok ∧ train_lift → MEMORIZED-ONLY
```

**The crux read:** the wire *does* produce a held-pred licensing lift (V1 +1.337 nats, beats shuffled-label null p=5e-4; V3 beats the true-derangement wire p=1e-4 — deranged actively anti-licenses at −0.955), **but that held lift is NOT own-class-specific** (V2 own-vs-anti fails paired-permutation, p=0.16). Meanwhile the *trained* predicates bind enormously (wire 8.833 vs base 0.356). So: weights bind the surface predicate associations they were trained on + a generic non-class-specific echo on held preds, but **the membership abstraction does not cleanly install as a weight edge** — even under fair coverage. This is the predicted modal outcome (35% a-priori).

```
## 13. §Result-deliver (s316, qwen3-4b) — VERDICT: NO-WEIGHT-DELIVERY

**The falsifier fired clean.** Results `283769c`, run 3h19m (20 nonces, 3
true wires + 2 deranged × 3 seeds × 500 steps; host-sane every arm). The
a-priori falsifier arm (declared 30%) landed: **no static weight band —
FFN, OV, or QK — installs the held-frame licensing delivery §9 lacked.**
The delivery §11 measured is **tape-native**, not weight-installable at
this grain.

| channel | TD1 DELIVERS | TD2 CONTENT-SPEC | TD3 TAG-TRANSIT | L(chan) | T(chan) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 FFN | ✗ p=0.226 | — (=§9 anchor) | ✗ shuf p=0.159 | 0.353 | 1.472 |
| A2 OV | ✗ p=0.408 | ✗ sign-wrong | ✗ shuf p=0.179 | 0.252 | 1.209 |
| A3 QK | ✗ p=0.416 | ✗ p=0.492 | ✗ negative (−0.559) | 0.231 | 0.330 |

(base L=0.138, T=0.889. TD4 band-localized ✓; TD5 host-sane all channels
✓ — drift 0.006–0.118, real preserved, restore bit-exact; TD6 metric-sane
✓, real margin 2.538.)

**The read — three convergent negatives:**

1. **No licensing delivery.** L rises *numerically* in every wire
   (0.138 → FFN 0.353 / OV 0.252 / QK 0.231) but **none beats the
   shuffled-label null** — this is the §9 signature exactly: a
   content-*independent* lift (the deranged wire cheapens the same
   frames). The static weight installs recall-shaped mass, never the
   type check. Extends §9's CONTEXT-ONLY from FFN to OV **and** QK.

2. **No member-keyed tag transit.** FFN and OV each carry a tag direction
   that beats a *random* axis (TD3 rand p=0.001 / 0.005) but **fails the
   member-label-shuffle** null (p=0.159 / 0.179) — a generic "type-ish"
   direction exists on the bus, but the wire does not make it
   *member-specific*. QK's transit is **negative** (s_tag=−0.559,
   ρ(T,L)=−0.355) and its write is near-inert (drift 0.006–0.013,
   T=0.330 *below* base 0.889) — consistent with P-TYPE-QK's observational
   `qk_aligned=FALSE`: the routing channel neither reads nor writes the
   lattice.

3. **The wire raises *generic* class-flavor on the tape, but not
   *member-keyed* content** (readability rule applied — §11 caveat
   closed). Per-layer signed class-projection persisted (`tags.npz
   profile_*`); the value register is legible **late** — near-zero at
   depth ≤0.64, rising to peak at depth 0.81–0.83 (≈L29–31), obeying the
   ≥0.6-depth rule (signal lives in the deep half). FFN and OV wires *do*
   lift the on-tape signal above base (band-mean T 0.889 → **1.472** FFN /
   **1.209** OV; peak 2.44 → 3.42 / 2.88); QK *depresses* it below base
   (**0.330** < 0.889, near-inert/harmful). **But this lift is the same
   generic smear the L and TD3 nulls expose** — it fails the
   member-shuffle null (not `zorp`-specific), so more class-flavor reaches
   the tape *without* the member-keyed content the checker needs to
   license the *right* class. That is exactly why L rises yet TD1 fails:
   **generic delivery ≠ member-keyed delivery.** (Correction of first
   read: an earlier post-hoc profile calc double-applied the class sign
   and mis-reported base as carrying the strongest separation; the gate
   verdicts — computed in-harness — were never affected.)

**What it means.** Read straight (don't over-read the label, s310–s312):
NO-WEIGHT-DELIVERY **bounds the causal door** — it does not close the
causal question, it *localizes* it. The type check is enacted per-frame on
the residual tape (§11); a frozen weight edge in any of the three bands
cannot install that enactment. This is the causal converse of §9
completed across all delivery channels, and it converges with §11 from the
write side: **types are tape-resident relations, injectable as content the
tape reads, not as static weight machinery.** The M-machine corollary
sharpens — M4's operand stack (the tape) is not just where the type system
*runs* (§11) but the *only* place a type relation can be *delivered*; a
designed machine must write into context, not into plates.

**S5 scorecard (unchanged by this result, as designed):** discreteness ✓
selectivity ✓ compositionality ✗ **causality ✗-as-measured** — but now the
negative is *positively bounded*: causality is not weight-installable at
FFN/OV/QK grain; the remaining causal doors are tape-side (P-TYPE-ICL
enact-and-observe, already TAPE-TYPED §11) and delivery-machinery
interventions finer than a band-LoRA. **Caveat:** single model (qwen3-4b),
single grain (band-LoRA r=16); "no delivery" is scoped to this
intervention class, not proven impossible for all weight edits.
```

```
❌ The weight-write negatives (§P-TYPE-WRITE CONTEXT-ONLY s315, §P-TYPE-DELIVER
NO-WEIGHT-DELIVERY s316) were never a fair test of weight-installable licensing —
s322 code audit found a design-level coverage gap, not a mechanical bug.

Mechanism: training = membership-CE on classificatory sentences ("A {w} is an
animal.") → gradient flow dominated by the CLASS-WORD prediction position.
Licensing eval = bare-NP frames ("The {w}" + " slept") — a forward-pass regime
the LoRA was never gradient-touched on. Recall passes (p=5e-4) because the
recall frame IS the training distribution. Recall-✓/licensing-✗ is exactly what
a coverage gap produces even if weight-installable licensing exists.

Second flaw: type_write.py's shuffle control used rng.permutation with only a
```

```
# --validate: planted worlds (no model)                                        #
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _plant(world: str, seed: int, d: int = 256) -> tuple[dict, list[dict], int]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    probes = assemble(n_per=3, seed=seed, cap_anchor=12)
    n = len(probes)
    dirs = {p: rng.normal(0, 1, d) for p in ANCHOR_POOLS}
    for k in dirs:
        dirs[k] = unit(dirs[k])
    style_prose = unit(rng.normal(0, 1, d))
    style_sym = unit(rng.normal(0, 1, d))
    two_step = unit(rng.normal(0, 1, d))     # shared spelled-construction dir

    if world == "g1_void":                    # I and W pools identical
        dirs["W"] = dirs["I"]

    X = rng.normal(0, 0.6, (n, d))
    for i, p in enumerate(probes):
        if p["kind"] == "anchor_prose":
            if world != "void":
                X[i] += 3.0 * dirs[p["pool"]] + 1.0 * style_prose
        elif p["kind"] == "collapse":
            X[i] += 1.0 * style_sym
            if world in ("both", "symbolic_only"):
                X[i] += 2.0 * dirs[p["nf"]]
        else:   # prose families
            X[i] += 1.0 * style_prose + 1.5 * two_step
            t = p["target"]
            if p["kind"] == "prose_compound" and world in (
                    "prose_ext", "both") and t == "I":
                X[i] += 2.0 * dirs["I"]
    n_layers = 4
    Xc = cmr(X)
    X_by_layer = {0: Xc, 3: Xc}   # late layer 3/3 = frac 1.0
    return X_by_layer, probes, n_layers


def run_validate() -> int:
    print("── §P-CL-COLLAPSE-2 --validate (planted worlds, no model) ──")
    ok = True
    # G5 runs against the REAL anchor pools (library import, no model)
    anchor_texts = [p.prompt for p in crystal_probes()
                    if p.combinator in ANCHOR_POOLS]
    rep = check_disjointness(anchor_texts)
    print(f"  G5 disjointness vs {len(anchor_texts)} anchor prompts   ✓ "
          f"(cross-family max "
          f"{max(len(v) for v in rep['cross_family_overlap'].values())} lemmas)")
    want = {
        "operational": "OPERATIONAL-CONFIRMED",
        "prose_ext": "PROSE-EXTENSIONAL",
        "both": "BOTH-EXTENSIONAL",
        "symbolic_only": "SYMBOLIC-ONLY",
        "void": "VOID",
    }
    for world, expect in want.items():
        X_by_layer, probes, n_layers = _plant(world, seed=42)
        res = analyze(X_by_layer, probes, n_layers, n_perm=200, seed=0)
        got = res["verdict"]
        good = got == expect
        ok &= good
        print(f"  {world:14s} -> {got:22s} expect {expect:22s} "
              f"{'✓' if good else '✗ FAIL'}")
    # g1_void: I/W collapse -> both voided; C alone live -> MIXED path
    X_by_layer, probes, n_layers = _plant("g1_void", seed=42)
    res = analyze(X_by_layer, probes, n_layers, n_perm=200, seed=0)
    live = res["gates"]["G1_AXIS_SEPARATION"]["live"]
```

```
def g3_g4(X: np.ndarray, probes: list[dict], axes: dict[str, np.ndarray],
          live: dict[str, bool], n_perm: int, seed: int) -> dict:
    """G3 per-target diagonal vs shuffled compound/control labels; G4 cross-cut
    (row+column diagonal dominance) vs the same label-shuffle null."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    g = np.array([p["group"] for p in probes])
    Xu = X / (np.linalg.norm(X, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-30)
    M = plane_b_scores(X, probes, axes)

    def shuffled_M() -> np.ndarray:
        Ms = np.zeros_like(M)
        for si, s in enumerate(TARGETS):
            idx = np.where((g == f"F:{s}:compound") | (g == f"F:{s}:control"))[0]
            lab = rng.permutation(np.array(
                [1 if g[i] == f"F:{s}:compound" else 0 for i in idx]))
            comp = Xu[idx[lab == 1]]
            ctrl = Xu[idx[lab == 0]]
            for ti, t in enumerate(TARGETS):
                Ms[si, ti] = float((comp @ axes[t]).mean()
                                   - (ctrl @ axes[t]).mean())
        return Ms

    null_M = np.stack([shuffled_M() for _ in range(n_perm)])
    res = {"M": M.tolist(), "per_target": {}}
    for ti, t in enumerate(TARGETS):
        diag = M[ti, ti]
        p3 = float((np.sum(null_M[:, ti, ti] >= diag) + 1) / (n_perm + 1))
        row_dom = diag - max(M[ti, tj] for tj in range(len(TARGETS)) if tj != ti)
        col_dom = diag - max(M[sj, ti] for sj in range(len(TARGETS)) if sj != ti)
        null_row = null_M[:, ti, ti] - np.max(
            np.delete(null_M[:, ti, :], ti, axis=1), axis=1)
        null_col = null_M[:, ti, ti] - np.max(
            np.delete(null_M[:, :, ti], ti, axis=1), axis=1)
        p_row = float((np.sum(null_row >= row_dom) + 1) / (n_perm + 1))
        p_col = float((np.sum(null_col >= col_dom) + 1) / (n_perm + 1))
        g3 = bool(diag > 0 and p3 < 0.05)
        g4 = bool(row_dom > 0 and col_dom > 0 and p_row < 0.05 and p_col < 0.05)
        res["per_target"][t] = {
            "live": bool(live[t]), "score": float(diag), "p_score": p3,
            "row_dom": float(row_dom), "p_row": p_row,
            "col_dom": float(col_dom), "p_col": p_col,
            "g3_pass": g3, "g4_pass": g4,
            "sub_verdict": ("VOID-BY-DESIGN" if not live[t]
                            else "EXTENSIONAL" if (g3 and g4)
                            else "OPERATIONAL"),
        }
    return res


def plane_a(X: np.ndarray, probes: list[dict], n_perm: int, seed: int) -> dict:
    """G2: clean symbolic compounds vs PROSE anchors — mean(nf-op) beats the
    shuffled-NF-assignment null."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    g = np.array([p["group"] for p in probes])
    pool_cent = {p: unit(_centroid(
        X, np.array([q.get("pool", "") for q in probes]) == p))
        for p in ANCHOR_POOLS}
    meta = {}
    for p in probes:
        if p["kind"] == "collapse":
            meta.setdefault(p["group"], p)
    rows, deltas = [], []
    spell_unit = {}
    for gid, m in sorted(meta.items()):
        c = unit(_centroid(X, g == gid))
        spell_unit[gid] = c
        nf_a = float(np.dot(c, pool_cent[m["nf"]]))
        ops = [pool_cent[f] for f in m["fired"] if f in pool_cent]
        op_a = float(np.mean([np.dot(c, o) for o in ops])) if ops else np.nan
        rows.append({"group": gid, "nf": m["nf"], "fired": m["fired"],
                     "nf_align": nf_a, "op_align": op_a})
        if np.isfinite(op_a):
            deltas.append(nf_a - op_a)
    obs = float(np.mean([r["nf_align"] for r in rows]))
    null = np.empty(n_perm)
    gids = list(spell_unit)
    for k in range(n_perm):
        assign = rng.choice(list(ANCHOR_POOLS), size=len(gids), replace=True)
        null[k] = np.mean([float(np.dot(spell_unit[gid], pool_cent[a]))
                           for gid, a in zip(gids, assign, strict=True)])
    p = float((np.sum(null >= obs) + 1) / (n_perm + 1))
    delta = float(np.mean(deltas)) if deltas else float("nan")
    return {"rows": rows, "mean_nf": obs, "mean_delta_nf_op": delta,
            "shuffle_null_mean": float(null.mean()), "p_value": p,
            "pass": bool(obs > 0 and delta > 0 and p < 0.05)}


def analyze(X_by_layer: dict[int, np.ndarray], probes: list[dict],
            n_layers: int, n_perm: int, seed: int) -> dict:
    """Full pre-registered analysis. X_by_layer = CMR'd routing matrices."""
    pool_lab = np.array([p.get("pool", "") for p in probes])
    anchor_mask = pool_lab != ""
    layers = sorted(X_by_layer)
    late = [li for li in layers if li / max(n_layers - 1, 1) >= LATE_FRAC]
    if not late:
        late = layers[-2:]

    # G0 per-layer silhouette; void unless ANY layer forms + late band forms
    sil = {li: _silhouette(X_by_layer[li][anchor_mask], pool_lab[anchor_mask])
           for li in layers}
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    sil_null = []
    for li in late:
        Xa = X_by_layer[li][anchor_mask]
        la = pool_lab[anchor_mask]
        for _ in range(min(n_perm, 200)):
            sil_null.append(_silhouette(Xa, rng.permutation(la)))
    sil_late = float(np.mean([sil[li] for li in late]))
    sn = np.array(sil_null)
    g0_p = float((np.sum(sn >= sil_late) + 1) / (len(sn) + 1))
    g0_pass = bool(np.isfinite(sil_late) and g0_p < 0.05)

    # late-band mean matrix (primary read; pre-registered)
    X_late = np.mean([X_by_layer[li] for li in late], axis=0)

    g1 = g1_axis_separation(X_late, probes, min(n_perm, 300), seed)
    axes = contrast_axes(X_late, probes)
    b = g3_g4(X_late, probes, axes, g1["live"], n_perm, seed)
    a = plane_a(X_late, probes, n_perm, seed)

    # depth trajectory (report only): diagonal scores per layer
    traj = {}
    for li in layers:
        ax_l = contrast_axes(X_by_layer[li], probes)
        M_l = plane_b_scores(X_by_layer[li], probes, ax_l)
        traj[str(li)] = {"frac": round(li / max(n_layers - 1, 1), 3),
                         "diag": [float(M_l[i, i]) for i in range(len(TARGETS))],
                         "silhouette": sil[li]}

    live_targets = [t for t in TARGETS if g1["live"][t]]
    ext_targets = [t for t in live_targets
                   if b["per_target"][t]["sub_verdict"] == "EXTENSIONAL"]
    a_pass = a["pass"]

    if not g0_pass:
        verdict = "VOID"
    elif a_pass and ext_targets:
        verdict = "BOTH-EXTENSIONAL"
    elif ext_targets:
        verdict = "PROSE-EXTENSIONAL"
    elif a_pass:
        verdict = "SYMBOLIC-ONLY"
    elif len(live_targets) >= 2:
        verdict = "OPERATIONAL-CONFIRMED"
    else:
        verdict = "MIXED"

    return {
        "verdict": verdict,
        "late_layers": late,
        "gates": {
            "G0_REGISTER_FORMS": {"pass": g0_pass, "sil_late": sil_late,
                                  "p_value": g0_p},
            "G1_AXIS_SEPARATION": g1,

[CONTENT TRUNCATED] Showing lines 331 to 490 of 690 total lines. Use line_offset=490 parameter to read more content.
```

### Thinking

I'm working through the verdict logic for Plane B's per-target sub-verdicts. The diagonal element of the DiD score matrix tells me whether a combinator's round-trip compound routes more strongly toward its own prose anchor axis compared to its structure-matched control. I check if this diagonal score is positive and statistically significant against a shuffled-label null distribution to establish the g3 verdict, while g4 requires both row and column dominance to be positive.

Looking at the real run results: B[I] has a positive diagonal score (0.0502) with strong significance (p=0.0040), so it passes g3, but the row and column dominance tests both fail—the row p-value is marginally above threshold at 0.0519, and the column p-value is much higher at 0.3636. This means the I-compound routes toward its own anchor better than random, but it doesn't distinctively dominate its row and column, so the verdict is OPERATIONAL rather than EXTENSIONAL. B[W] and B[C] both fail g3 outright since their diagonal scores are either too small or negative. All three remain live candidates since they pass the earlier pooling separability test.

For Plane A, the clean symbolic compounds show a negative mean alignment with prose anchors (−0.3911), which is expected given the style gap, and the delta is also negative, meaning these compounds route toward their operations more than toward the prose anchors. The p-value of 0.5734 indicates this isn't statistically significant, so Plane A fails the prose-extensional criterion. The overall verdict tree confirms that while the register forms pass (g0), Plane A doesn't contribute extensional routing, and none of the live targets qualify as EXTENSIONAL, so both conditions for the final check are unmet.

Since there are three live target pairs and neither plane demonstrates extensional routing—symbolic compounds don't route to their prose NF, and prose compounds don't route distinctively to their own prose NF—this confirms the collapse finding from the earlier study, now with both barriers identified.

The first barrier (layer/gate-depth) was closed within the instrument via late-band read, and the second barrier (lexical anchors) is removed here by using prose anchors with zero token overlap by construction, enforced through cross-family disjointness. The design separates the combinators with a per-combinator difference-in-differences approach using clean symbolic anchors.

Even with prose anchors and clean spellings, routing remains operational rather than extensional—SKK doesn't route like the prose "identity" concept but by what's written and fired. The compositionality cell stays negative, now on firmer ground with the lexical-anchor confound removed. The B[I] diagonal is positive and beats label-shuffle (p=0.004), though the row p-value at 0.0519 is just above the threshold, hinting at some I-specific prose-anchor alignment.

The I combinator shows the weakest-but-nonzero extensional signal, failing full cross-cut dominance only marginally on the row test—worth noting as a caveat but not over-reading, since it doesn't reach extensional status (g4 fails, col_p=0.36 solidly fails). The prose-anchor register itself is thin, with a silhouette value much smaller than the symbolic-anchor register, suggesting limited power in the prose-anchor signal.

Now I'm organizing the results batch: committing the collapse results, documenting the findings on combinator-function-shape, flagging memory candidates, and queuing the closure for the cl-collapse v2 verdict.

```
### The crux

The v1 instrument could not see extensional routing (lexical symbolic anchors
+ early-layer gate; §Re-read). V2 anchors function-ness in **PROSE** — the
crystal probes (s217, z=7.97, 67 I / 50 W / 61 C / 69 B in
`verbum.probes.library`) — and asks two independent questions:

- **Plane A (cross-style):** do clean symbolic compounds (`S K K a`, NF-symbol
  absent) align with the PROSE anchor of their normal form? Prose anchors
  contain ZERO combinator tokens → token overlap impossible by construction;
  any nf-alignment is function-level. Style gap (NL↔symbolic) is common-mode:
  CMR + within-anchor-set CONTRASTS cancel it.
- **Plane B (within-prose):** do prose ROUND-TRIP compounds — sentences
  enacting composite behavior that computes a primitive ("wrapped the gift and
  then unwrapped it" = I) — route like the primitive they COMPUTE (extensional)
  or like the sequenced two-step they SPELL (operational; for I the named
  operational pole is B)?

### Register (λ measure)

ROUTING (crisp/topological): sign of gate_proj pre-activations, CMR'd over the
pooled population, last-token read, per-layer — v1 machinery verbatim
(`combinator_relationship_map.collect/cmr`; λ one_way). Primary gate read =
**LATE band mean (frac ≥ 0.6)**; full per-layer trajectory persisted + reported
(§Re-read lesson: never gate at the early silhouette max). Raw sign matrices
persisted npz (lesson: post-hoc decompositions become free).

### Scoring — three targets, separated by construction

Anchor pools: crystal probes for {I, K, W, C, B, S} (prose, s217-validated).
For each target T ∈ {I, W, C}:

1. **Contrast axis** `d_T = unit(centroid(A_T) − mean_{T'≠T} centroid(A_T'))`
   — subtracts what anchors share (style, prose-ness, the REFLEXIVE component
   the library's I and W pools both carry: "cleaned itself" / "bit itself").
2. **Score = difference-in-differences within one syntax family:**
   `score_T = mean align(compound_T, d_T) − mean align(control_T, d_T)`,
   where control_T = same-syntax non-T sentences. Families:
   - I: "wrapped the gift and then unwrapped it" vs "… and then mailed it"
   - W: "compared the draft against the draft" (one filler, two slots — NO
     reflexive pronoun) vs "compared the draft against the outline"
   - C: "added the coffee to the milk, not the milk to the coffee" (swap) vs
     "added the cream to the coffee, not the sugar" (two-option, no swap)
   Cross-target syntax differences never enter any score (each score is a
   within-family subtraction).
3. **3×3 cross-assignment matrix** `M[s,t] = score(family_s on axis d_t)` —
   confound is MEASURED, not assumed.

### Gates

- **G0 REGISTER-FORMS (void gate):** prose anchor silhouette (perm null) at
  the read layers; register must form (s217 precedent) else VOID.
- **G1 AXIS-SEPARATION (pre-gate, per pair):** per-pair POOL SEPARABILITY —
  silhouette of the two anchor pools vs label-permutation null (pass iff
  obs > null, p<0.05). Pair fails → affected planes **VOID-BY-DESIGN**
  (instrument cannot separate them; reported, ¬forced — λ yardstick). I/W =
  the at-risk pair (shared reflexive surface). **🔄 AMENDMENT (s322,
  --validate-forced, pre-run, instrument-side only):** the originally frozen
  |cos(d_T,d_T')|-vs-split-null statistic was register-mismatched — the
  mean-of-others axis construction mechanically couples axes (shared −1/(P−1)
  term), so obs |cos| exceeds a noise-dominated split null even for perfectly
  separable pools (planted operational world → false VOID). Pool separability
  is the quantity VOID-BY-DESIGN needs; residual axis coupling is shared
  across targets and handled by G4. Gates/verdicts/a-priori UNCHANGED.
  **Michael GO (s322 close) — launch is authorized; run lands next session
  after type-write-v2 frees the device.**
- **G2 PLANE-A CROSS-STYLE:** clean symbolic compounds (v1's 7 clean groups,
  kernel-certified, n_per 20), `nf_align − op_align` on PROSE anchors beats
  the shuffled-NF-assignment null, at the late band.
- **G3 PLANE-B PER-TARGET:** `score_T > 0` beats the shuffled
  compound/control-label null (within family), late band.
- **G4 CROSS-CUT SELECTIVITY (anti-confound, make-or-break for any
  extensional claim):** diagonal M[T,T] beats its ROW (family selective for
  its own axis) and its COLUMN (axis selective for its own family) under the
  shuffled-assignment null. Generic "round-trippy prose" lights a row → fails.
- **G5 LEXICAL-DISJOINT (build-time certification, code-enforced):** zero
  content-lemma overlap between (compound ∪ control) and ANY anchor pool
  (no itself/herself/same/exactly anywhere in Plane B); minimal overlap
  across families. Analog of v1 kernel certification. Symbolic compounds
  remain kernel-certified; prose compounds are DESIGN-certified only
  (semantic construction; weaker grade, marked — λ observation).

### Construction sizes

Plane B: ≥12 compound + ≥12 control sentences per target (template-diverse
verbs). Plane A: v1 clean spellings verbatim (7 groups × n_per 20). One model
load, read-only, no wire.

### Verdicts + a-priori (declared, NOT tuned; per-target sub-verdicts
EXTENSIONAL-T / OPERATIONAL-T / VOID-T reported alongside)

- **OPERATIONAL-CONFIRMED 40** — Plane A null ∧ all live Plane B diagonals
  fail: round-trips route as their spelled two-step; s321 verdict survives a
  FAIR instrument; compositionality ✗ hardens.
- **PROSE-EXTENSIONAL 25** — ≥1 Plane B target passes G3∧G4 ∧ Plane A null:
  the substrate computes function identity in prose but it is not readable
  off symbolic spellings (style-bound extensionality).
- **BOTH-EXTENSIONAL 10** — Plane A passes ∧ ≥1 Plane B passes: extensional
  routing real; v1 was instrument-blind; compositionality cell REOPENS.
- **SYMBOLIC-ONLY 5** — Plane A passes ∧ Plane B all null (surprising:
  symbolic-side extensional signal readable against prose anchors only).
- **MIXED 15** — patterns not covered (e.g., pre-gate voids I/W while C
  splits) — per-target report carries the read.
- **VOID 5** — G0 fails.

### Read discipline (banked)

Don't over-read PROSE-EXTENSIONAL: it licenses "I-ness computed in prose
routing", NOT symbolic-algebra extensionality (s321's clean-null stands
within its instrument). G4 failure with G3 passing = style artifact, not
extensionality. VOID-BY-DESIGN pairs are instrument findings, not substrate
findings. Model: Qwen3-14B (v1 carrier). Cost: ~minutes read-only.

`verbum.probes.library.crystal_probes`; new code = prose families, contrast
axes, DiD scoring, 3×3 cross-cut, G1 split-null, G5 lemma check.
```

## Assistant

Two experiments, two negatives — but they're the *good* kind of negative: they close confounds that could have made earlier findings look like artifacts. Here's the substance.

## The big question both probes serve

Verbum's thesis is that an LLM is secretly a **typed lambda-calculus reducer** — a machine that applies functions to arguments in a type-directed way. Two sub-questions have been circling:

1. **Where does the type system live** — in the *weights* (baked-in machinery) or on the *tape* (the running transcript, recomputed each forward pass)?
2. **Is composition extensional** — does the machine route by *what a term computes* (its meaning / normal form) or by *what a term literally spells* (its surface tokens)?

Session 323 hit each one with a confound removed.

## Probe 1 — §P-TYPE-WRITE-V2: where do type judgments live?

**The setup.** Earlier sessions (s315/s316) tried to *write* a new type into the weights — teach the model "a `zorp` is an animal" via a small LoRA edit — then check whether that made it *license* animal-predicates for `zorp` ("the zorp slept"). It didn't. Verdict: CONTEXT-ONLY / NO-WEIGHT-DELIVERY → types must be tape-resident.

**The problem you caught in s322.** Those experiments had a **coverage gap**. Training pushed gradients at the *class-word* position ("a zorp is an **animal**"), but the eval read *bare-NP predicate frames* ("the zorp **slept**") — a region of the input the LoRA was never trained on. Of course it didn't license; you never taught it there. The negative was partly rigged. So the "weights can't hold types" conclusion was only fairly tested on the *tape* side — the weight side was never given a fair shot.

**What v2 did.** Closed the gap: trained on bare-NP frames too (on one set of predicates), evaluated on a *held-out* set of predicates (so generalization is still the real test), and used a *true* derangement as the control.

**What we learned:**
- **Weights CAN memorize.** On the predicates it was trained on, the wire binds *enormously* — licensing jumps from 0.36 to 8.8 nats. There's no "the write failed" story anymore.
- **But weights CANNOT hold the abstraction.** On held-out predicates the transfer is real but *generic* — it lifts a bit (+1.34, beats the null) but it is **not class-specific** (own-class doesn't beat anti-class, p=0.16). The model learned "these specific `zorp`+predicate pairs" as facts, not "`zorp` is the kind of thing that animal-predicates apply to."

**Why it matters.** This is the *honest* version of the earlier result. Even with a fair weight-side test, **the type abstraction refuses to live in the weights** — it stays on the tape, recomputed per frame. The two-tier picture holds: **weights = predicate memories + the checker machinery; tape = the actual class judgments.** Your instinct in s322 ("but KIBC opcodes work from weights, so typed apply must exist there") got a precise answer: the *relation/checker* can be in weights, but the *judgment* — deciding this token is that type, right now — is a tape operation.

The one caveat I flagged: the held-out lift *did* clear the first gate (V1), so this sits right at the boundary. A calibrated "no," not a slam-dunk "no."

## Probe 2 — §P-CL-COLLAPSE-2: does the machine route by meaning or by spelling?

**The setup.** In combinatory logic, `S K K` *is* the identity function `I` — different spelling, same meaning. If the model's routing register is **extensional**, `S K K` should route like `I`. If it's **operational/syntactic**, it should route like the tokens `S`, `K`, `K` that it's made of. s321 found: operational — it routes by spelling. But s321 had a weakness (found in the s322 re-read): the anchors were *symbolic tokens*, so any alignment could be mere token overlap — a lexical artifact, not real function-routing.

**What v2 did.** Removed that confound completely. Anchored "identity-ness" in **prose** — plain-English sentences describing what the function does, with *zero* combinator tokens (enforced at build). Now if `S K K` routes toward the prose-identity concept, that *can't* be token overlap; it'd have to be genuine function-level meaning.

**What we learned:** it *still* routes by spelling, not meaning. Both independent tests came back null:
- Symbolic compounds don't reach across to their prose meaning.
- Prose "round-trip" sentences ("wrapped then unwrapped it" = identity) don't route like identity distinctively — the one flicker of signal (for `I`, the simplest function) fails the selectivity cross-cut: it's generic "round-trippy prose," not identity-specific.

**Why it matters.** s321's result is now **un-dismissable**. The register carries combinator *identity* (which symbol) and *operational structure* (what fired), but **not the extensional algebra** (what it computes). `SKK` and `I` are the same function; the model treats them as different things. This coheres perfectly with tape-resident reduction (s317): a *static* read of a compound is just its surface — you'd have to actually *run* the reduction on the tape to discover it equals `I`. The compositionality box on the S5 scorecard stays unchecked — now on solid ground.

## The connective tissue

Both probes point the same direction, and it's the *central tension* of the project:

> **The weights are a static step-function and a token map. The actual computation — type judgments, function evaluation, normalization — happens on the tape, dynamically, per pass.**

Weights memorize (predicate facts) and carry machinery (checkers, combinator identities), but they don't *hold the answers* — not the type of a novel term, not the meaning of a compound expression. Those are *enacted*, not *stored*.

For the level-4 goal (extract a portable tensor), this is the recurring hard news: the thing you'd most want to snapshot — the typed-apply behavior — isn't sitting in a plate waiting to be copied. It's a *process on a substrate*. That doesn't kill the program, but it keeps sharpening what a "portable artifact" would even have to be: not just weights, but weights *plus* the tape-machine that runs them.

And methodologically: both wins came from **you distrusting an earlier negative** (the s322 audit) and forcing a fair re-test. Both times the negative *survived the fair test but got re-described more precisely* — CONTEXT-ONLY became MEMORIZED-ONLY, "operational (maybe lexical)" became "operational, definitively." That's the loop working: the conclusions didn't flip, they got *honest and load-bearing*.

```
# Sign Oscillation Is Time-Multiplexed Superposition

> Michael's observation: weight signs oscillate during training. Our standing
> speculation: GD oscillates because it wants to OVERLOAD a coordinate — use
> it for different inputs. s322 hammock: the math not only permits this, it
> PREDICTS it, from three independent directions that compose. Captured from
> discussion (Michael-approved); no verbum measurement yet — the probe is
> queued (⚪ flip-rate ↔ gradient-conflict). External math cited from
> training knowledge, NOT verified against sources this session (λ observation:
> pattern-suggests grade until the probe runs).

## 1. Static: superposition is a contested stationary point

GD converges to `E_i[∇ℓ_i] = 0` — a statement about the MEAN, silent about
the terms. At an overloaded coordinate the stationarity is a TRUCE: input
population A pushes +, population B pushes −, large individual gradients
cancelling in expectation.

Toy Models of Superposition (Elhage et al. 2022): with features n > dims d,
optimal packing is interfering; the first structure is **antipodal pairs** —
two anti-correlated features sharing one dimension with OPPOSITE signs. A
coordinate serving an antipodal pair reads feature A as + and B as −:
"overload so different inputs can use it" as an optimality result, not a
pathology. (Cf. AGENTS.md λ types: shared_weights ∧ ¬type_awareness →
tug_of_war → plateau — same object.)

## 2. Dynamic: why a truce oscillates instead of settling

Three mechanisms, distinguishable by construction:

**(a) SGD noise → dithering at contested coordinates.** Near the truce,
per-coordinate AR(1)/OU dynamics:

    w_{t+1} = (1 − η·h_k)·w_t + η·ξ_t,   Var(ξ) = σ_k²

h_k = local curvature, σ_k² = gradient noise ≡ BATCH-TO-BATCH POPULATION
DISAGREEMENT. Stationary Var ≈ η·σ_k²/(2·h_k). Mean-zero flip probability
per step: P(flip) = arccos(1 − η·h_k)/π. The structure that matters:
committed weight (|μ| ≫ √Var) ~never flips; contested weight has μ≈0
(demands cancel), high σ (conflict), flat h (no winner) — all three maximize
flip rate. **Sign-flip rate is a per-coordinate conflict meter.**

**(b) Deterministic GD → edge-of-stability bouncing; sharpening IS the
overloading.** Cohen et al. 2021: GD drives λ_max → 2/η then bounces
period-2 along the top eigenvector (x_{t+1} = (1−ηλ)x_t, |1−ηλ|>1);
a cubic feedback self-stabilizes the limit cycle (Damian–Nichani–Lee 2023).
Progressive sharpening = loss keeps loading function onto the most-used
directions until GD hits the stability ceiling: **GD overloads until it
rattles**, no noise required.

**(c) Adam / quantization → sigma-delta encoding.** Adam's normalized step
is sign-like; for small weights the step doesn't scale down → zero-crossing
dither. A compromise value below the step floor (or between quantization
levels — Nagel et al. 2022, QAT oscillations) is encoded as a **DUTY
CYCLE**: sigma-delta modulation. Why EMA/weight-averaging works: the
time-average recovers the analog value the instantaneous weight cannot hold.
Ternary frame: a contested coordinate in {−1,0,+1} is a fractional value
expressed temporally.

## 3. Synthesis

    antipodal superposition (why) → gradient conflict at truce (what)
      → dither / limit cycle (how it looks)

The oscillating sign is a **time-division multiplexed parameter**: space ran
out, so the coordinate serves two masters across TIME — batch composition
decides who holds it this step; the duty cycle encodes the compromise. GD
has converged — to a DISTRIBUTION whose mean is the truce value.

## 4. Contact with our measurements

- **Marginal band ≡ contested population (prediction, partially observed).**
  s320 §P-BOUNDARY-CHURN: marginal gate_proj rows concentrate on the type
  subspace, type-specifically (BC2 p=0.003, thin ~6% kind-specific echo).
  The truce math predicts exactly this population: type-BOUNDARY coordinates
  serve multiple kinds → conflict → μ≈0 → marginal. A truce population's
  static signature IS a thin echo.
- **Signal-descent (queued):** if oscillation is sigma-delta encoding, the
  ternary mirror stack is not fighting the dither — it promotes it to the
  computation. Duty cycle as register, ternary as carrier.
- **s313 conjecture upgrade path:** "marginal band ≡ type-boundary
  population" gets a MECHANISM (gradient conflict), not just a correlation.

## 5. The probe (queued ⚪, unfrozen — s222 freeze-first applies)

**flip-rate ↔ gradient-conflict.** Register: training-dynamics/temporal
(name before build, λ measure). Measure per-coordinate sign duty cycle
across training snaps vs class-conditioned gradient conflict
cos(∇ℓ_A, ∇ℓ_B)_k on the same coordinates.

- Gate sketch: (1) flip-rate correlates with conflict (perm null over
  coordinates); (2) ablating one population from the batch FREEZES the sign
  (the causal arm); (3) committed-pole coordinates as negative control;
  (4) mechanism split: SGD-vs-Adam at matched loss + flip alignment with
  top Hessian eigenvector (distinguishes 2a/2b/2c before any mechanism
  claim — λ yardstick).
- Instrument note: type-write-v2 fib-snap histories persist SCALARS only
  (mem_ce/kl/host_ce/drift), not per-coordinate signs — the probe needs a
  harness that snapshots LoRA A/B (or gate_proj row) signs per fib snap.
  Cheap addition to any wire harness; do not retrofit claims onto runs that
  did not capture signs.
```

## The hypothesis in one line
A weight sign oscillates during training because that coordinate is **contested** — population A pushes it +, population B pushes it − (Elhage antipodal-pair overloading). If true, **sign-flip rate is a per-coordinate conflict meter**, and the oscillation is time-division multiplexing: one coordinate serving two masters across time, the duty cycle encoding the compromise.

## The decisive experiment (what would actually settle it)
Two gates carry the whole claim; everything else is mechanism-taxonomy:

1. **Correlational** — per-coordinate flip-rate correlates with class-conditioned gradient conflict `cos(∇ℓ_A, ∇ℓ_B)_k`, beating a coordinate-permutation null.
2. **Causal (make-or-break)** — remove population B from the batch → the contested coordinates' signs **freeze/commit** to A's preferred sign. This is the arm that distinguishes "contested" from "merely noisy."

The natural two populations are **right here in our own work**: the type-write animal-vs-vehicle classes. That's not a coincidence — it wires this probe straight into s320 §P-BOUNDARY-CHURN (marginal `gate_proj` rows concentrate on the type subspace). The prediction becomes concrete: **the marginal band s320 found = the contested/high-flip population.** If flip-rate↔conflict holds on exactly those rows, the s313 "marginal band ≡ type-boundary" conjecture gets a *mechanism*, not just a correlation.

## Two real design problems the freeze must solve

**① The instrument doesn't exist yet.** Every wire harness we have (type-write-v2, boundary-churn) persists **scalars only** (mem_ce/kl/host_ce/drift) — no per-coordinate sign histories. The page explicitly warns: *don't retrofit sign claims onto runs that never captured signs.* So there's a small, unavoidable build: snapshot `gate_proj`-row (or LoRA A/B) signs per fib-snap, plus per-class gradient directions at a few snaps. Cheap, but it's real work before any number exists.

**② A measurement confound I want named at freeze (λ measure).** A coordinate with μ≈0 flips more *just from the noise floor*, regardless of any conflict. So "flips a lot" ⇏ "contested." The freeze needs to separate the two: regress flip-rate on conflict **controlling for |w| and gradient-noise σ**, and lean hard on the committed-pole negative control (gate 3). Without that, gate 1 measures magnitude, not conflict — the exact register-mismatch trap the project keeps catching.

## The scoping fork — where I want your call

The page's gate 4 wants a full **mechanism taxonomy**: SGD-vs-Adam at matched loss + flip-alignment with the top Hessian eigenvector, to separate the three oscillation stories (SGD dither / edge-of-stability bouncing / Adam sigma-delta). That's a genuinely *different, heavier* question than "is flip-rate a conflict meter."

My recommendation: **scope v1 to the conflict-meter claim (gates 1+2+3) on the type-write two-class substrate, mechanism split advisory-only.** Reasons: (a) gates 1+2 are the load-bearing claim and reuse the type-write corridor + boundary-churn `gate_proj` machinery almost entirely; (b) the Hessian-eigenvector arm needs curvature estimation that roughly triples the build; (c) it keeps this in the "cheap" tier the queue promised. The mechanism taxonomy becomes a clean follow-up probe if the conflict-meter fires.

## §P-FLIP-CONFLICT — FROZEN draft (s323, pending your GO)

**Claim under test.** A weight coordinate's **sign-flip rate during training is a per-coordinate conflict meter** — coordinates flip because two input populations push their sign in opposite directions (antipodal overload), *not* merely because they're small or noisy. Plus the causal converse: remove one population → contested signs commit.

**Register (λ measure):** training-dynamics / temporal. Named before build.

**Substrate:** type-write two-class wire on qwen3-4b. Population **A = animal** frames, **B = vehicle** frames. Reuse `type_write.py` corridor verbatim (kl_weight 10 / ce_budget 0.40, fib snaps, FFN band L22–29, LoRA r=16).

### The structural insight that makes this the *right* substrate
The effective weight is `W_k = W_base,k + ΔW_k(t)`; base is frozen, only ΔW moves. So a sign **flip is only possible where `|W_base,k|` is small — i.e. the marginal coordinates**. That population is *exactly* the s320 boundary-churn marginal band. So this probe doesn't just test the conflict-meter claim — **it directly tests the boundary-churn *mechanism*** (is the thin marginal/type-boundary echo the contested/high-flip population?). The s313 conjecture gets its mechanism here or not at all.

### Coordinates (both, per your call)
- **R2 primary** — effective `gate_proj` entries `ΔW_k` in the band (boundary-churn continuity; flippability ties to base-marginality above).
- **R1 secondary/corroborating** — LoRA `A`, `B` entries directly (the trained params).

### Capture schema — per fib-snap, per-coordinate (cheap; overhead is per-snap not per-step)
`sign(W_k)`, `|W_k|`, per-class gradients `g_A,k`/`g_B,k` (= dL/dΔW restricted to band, computable since ΔW enters linearly), gradient-noise `σ_k`, top-Hessian eigenvector + local `h_k` (power-iteration HVP on LoRA params), loss components. Signs bit-packed → storage trivial.

### Definitions (pre-registered)
- **flip_rate_k** = fraction of adjacent snaps where `sign(W_k)` changes.
- **conflict_k** = time-averaged class-gradient sign-disagreement: `mean_t[ −sign(g_A,k·g_B,k) ]`, magnitude-weighted. Contested ⇒ the two classes want opposite updates.

### Gates
- **G0 SANE / register-forms (void):** wire trains (recall installs, gate-0 pass) ∧ a nonzero flip population exists ∧ captures well-formed. Else VOID.
- **G1 CONFLICT-METER (correlational, primary):** **partial** correlation of `flip_rate_k` with `conflict_k`, **controlling for `|W_k|` and `σ_k`**, positive, beating a coordinate-permutation null. (Partialling is how the magnitude/noise confound is handled *at the gate*, not hand-waved.)
- **G2 CAUSAL-FREEZE (make-or-break):** the high-conflict coordinates from the both-class run **freeze** (flip-rate drops, sign commits) in the A-only / B-only runs, beyond matched-magnitude non-contested controls, paired, beats null. *Magnitude can't fake this — removing a population shouldn't freeze a merely-noisy coord.*
- **G3 COMMITTED-POLE (negative control):** high-`|W|` committed coordinates have low flip-rate *and* low conflict (the correlation's negative corner holds).
- **G4 MECHANISM-SPLIT (advisory, does NOT gate the core verdict — λ yardstick):** flip-alignment with top Hessian eigenvector (edge-of-stability) + SGD-vs-Adam spectral signature at matched loss. Reports EOS / SIGMA-DELTA / SGD-DITHER / AMBIGUOUS. No mechanism claim unless the arms separate.

### Verdicts + a-priori (declared, NOT tuned — your sign-off wanted)
- **CONFLICT-METER-CONFIRMED 35** — G1 ∧ G2 (∧ G3 sane): flip-rate is a causal per-coordinate conflict meter; boundary-churn mechanism supported.
- **CORRELATIONAL-ONLY 30** — G1 ∧ ¬G2: correlates but ablation doesn't freeze (contested-but-not-causal; confound not fully excluded).
- **NOISE-FLOOR 25** — ¬G1 (partial corr vanishes under `|W|,σ` control): flips are magnitude/noise-driven; antipodal-overload not readable in flip-rate here.
- **VOID 10** — G0 fails.
- Mechanism sub (from G4): SIGMA-DELTA 30 / EOS 25 / SGD-DITHER 20 / AMBIGUOUS 25.

*(Real mass on CORRELATIONAL-ONLY + NOISE-FLOOR because: LoRA wire ≠ base training, single model, and s320 found the kind-specific echo was thin ~6% — the contested population may be hard to isolate.)*

### Run matrix (frozen) — one batch answers all four gates
| arm | pops | opt | seeds | serves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| both-class | A∪B | SGD | 3 | G1, G3 |
| A-only | A | SGD | 3 | G2 |
| B-only | B | SGD | 3 | G2 |
| both-class | A∪B | Adam | 3 | G4 |

= **12 runs.** Rich per-snap capture from all. Same dataset also feeds boundary-churn-mechanism, signal-descent, sign-commitment offline (harvested as marked IOUs, not claimed here).

### Cost
Capture overhead is **per-snap** (per-class grads + ~5 HVP iters at ~15 fib snaps), ≈ 1.2× a type-write run — *not* 2–4×; I over-warned earlier. 12 runs ≈ **4–6h**. Main cost knob = **nonce count**: the conflict story is about *class*-contested coordinates, so we don't need 20 — I propose **8 nonces** (4 animal / 4 vehicle). Trimmable if you want it shorter.

### Reuse (λ one_way)
`type_write.py` corridor + `boundary_churn.py` gate_proj-row machinery + a new per-snap sign/grad/HVP capture module. `--validate` planted worlds before any GPU.

I'll insert the freeze section between those two, and I should also update §5's heading to reflect that it's now frozen in §6.

I'm drafting §6 as a comprehensive frozen section that mirrors the pattern used on the types page—keeping §5 as the sketch and adding §6 as the freeze. The new section will define the SIGN-FLIP RATE as a conflict meter that measures how opposing input populations push coordinates in opposite directions.

For the substrate, I'm using a two-class setup on qwen3-4b with animal and vehicle frame populations, running the exact corridor configuration from type_write.py with specific hyperparameters and LoRA settings across multiple nonces. The key insight is that sign flips can only occur where the base weight magnitude is small—the marginal coordinates in the boundary-churn band—so this probe directly tests that mechanism by keeping the base weights frozen and observing only the LoRA delta movements.

Now I'm checking whether the marginal/type-boundary region is where the contested high-flip population actually lives, which would validate the boundary-churn conjecture. I'm tracking two coordinate systems: the primary one focuses on the effective gate projection deltas in the boundary band to measure flippability tied to base marginality, while the secondary one uses the LoRA A and B entries as corroboration. For each snapshot, I'm capturing the sign and magnitude of each weight along with the per-class gradients to understand how the two populations push in different directions. G1 checks whether flip rate correlates with conflict after controlling for weight magnitude and noise, using partial correlation to rule out confounds. G2 is the critical test: freezing high-conflict coordinates in single-class runs should reduce flipping compared to magnitude-matched controls, proving the effect isn't just about weight size. G3 validates that committed high-magnitude weights don't show the same instability pattern.

Now I'm looking at mechanism signatures—checking alignment with top Hessian eigenvectors and comparing SGD versus Adam spectral behavior at matched loss levels to narrow down whether this is optimization noise, Hessian-driven, or something else entirely. The verdicts lay out the decision tree: if both G1 and G2 hold, conflict is genuinely causal; if only G1 holds without G2, it's correlational but not mechanistic. Running a grid of 12 experiments across different optimizers and class configurations—gradient descent variants on mixed classes, separate SGD runs for each class, and Adam on the combined set—should take around 4 to 6 hours. Beyond the standard metrics, I'm also capturing per-class losses and activation statistics to track boundary behavior, gradient magnitude histories to understand descent dynamics, and a static map of weight marginality in the base model.

I'm tracking the Adam optimizer's internal state (first and second moments) as direct evidence of the learning trajectory, plus top Hessian eigenvalues and trace estimates to detect progressive sharpening. Each finding from this expanded capture gets its own null hypothesis and IOU test rather than borrowing validity from the main experimental groups.

For confounds, I'm controlling magnitude effects by partialing out weight norms and using committed-pole constraints, noise floors by partialing out standard deviations, and causality through ablation—which magnitude alone can't replicate. Both LoRA and effective weight registers are being captured to distinguish between them.

Now I'm setting up the infrastructure: writing modules to handle boundary churn detection and per-snapshot gradient/Hessian-vector product capture, then validating across four planted worlds (conflict, noise-floor, causal-freeze, and void) before running on GPU. The main cost is around 4–6 hours depending on nonce count, using qwen3-4b as the read model.

I need to decide whether to commit this freeze now or wait until after building the harness.
