The harness works end-to-end (train → hooks capture per-class effective-ΔW gradients → HVP runs on MPS → gates → restore). But calibrating the smoke exposed a genuine ambiguity in the frozen §6 that changes the science, so I stopped rather than silently pick.

### What the smoke revealed
The frozen §6 said R2 = "effective gate_proj entries ΔW_k" but its *structural pin* discussed the **effective weight** `W_base+ΔW` (flips gated by |W_base|). Empirically:

| flip register | late flip population | usable? |
|---|---|---|
| effective `W_base+ΔW` | **0.3–0.4%** (SGD), 1.6% (Adam) | ✗ too rare — probe lands uninformative VOID |
| delta `sign(ΔW)` + burn-in | **~9%** healthy, varied | ✓ |

Effective-weight sign flips are **intrinsically rare in a frozen-base LoRA wire** — ΔW is a small perturbation that almost never overpowers |W_base|, even in the bottom-8% band. Dense (per-step) sampling didn't help — it's a magnitude fact, not a Nyquist fact. The frozen framing would give the phenomenon no fair chance to appear.

The **delta's own sign** `sign(ΔW_k)` *is* the direct antipodal/sigma-delta quantity: a contested coord has ΔW dithering around 0 (μ≈0, high flip-rate) — exactly the predicted signature. With a **burn-in** (drop the B=0 cold-start where everything dithers), the late flip-rate is healthy and varied.

### Proposed amendment (instrument-side, pre-run; gates/verdicts/a-priori UNCHANGED)
1. **Primary flip register → `sign(ΔW)`** (delta), not effective `W_base+ΔW`. Capture effective-weight flips as a **secondary** reading.
2. **Burn-in 0.4** on the flip-rate window (drop cold-start dither); dense per-step sign capture.
3. **G3 committed-pole re-based on |ΔW|** (committed = large stable delta, low flip/conflict); **|W_base| kept as a reported covariate** — the boundary-churn tie-in becomes a *secondary correlation* (does flip-rate rise where |W_base| is small?), not the primary gate.
4. **lr_sgd = 0.1** (default 0.02 was too slow to move ΔW).

### The scientific cost — the part I want you to weigh
This **demotes the "flippable ≡ marginal band" structural pin from primary to a covariate reading.** The core conflict-meter claim (G1 partial-corr, G2 causal-freeze) is tested *more faithfully* on the delta, but the direct boundary-churn-**mechanism** test weakens to "is high flip-rate associated with small |W_base|?" — reported, not gated. a-priori (35/30/25/10) and all gates stay as frozen.

Three ways you might call it:
1. **Delta primary + effective/|W_base| secondary** (my recommendation) — tests the oscillation phenomenon faithfully, keeps boundary-churn as a covariate. 
2. **Keep effective (honor the pin), accept VOID risk** — scientifically purest to the freeze, but likely uninformative (≤0.4% flips).
3. **Delta primary but keep G3 on |W_base|** — preserves the boundary-churn gate as-is on top of the delta register (hybrid).

Which way? On your GO I'll record the amendment on the §6 freeze (runtime-forced, pre-run), commit the harness, and launch the 12-run matrix (~4–6h, tmux).

The G3 basis change affected the "correlational_only" planted world in an unexpected way — G2 is now passing with p=0.005 when it shouldn't be, but I didn't modify G2's logic. I'm trying to understand why the planted world's flip_s parameter changed when only G3 was adjusted. Wait, I need to trace through the `correlational_only` setup more carefully. The `flip_s` calculation uses the same `flip` array but subtracts 0.1 and adds fresh noise, so `delta = flip - flip_s` ends up being roughly 0.1 minus that noise term. The key question is whether this delta correlates with conflict, since if it does, that would explain why G2 is picking up a signal when it shouldn't.

But here's the catch with clipping: high-conflict coordinates have high flip values (around 0.9), so flip_s lands around 0.8, giving delta ≈ 0.1. Low-conflict coordinates have low flip values (around 0.2), so flip_s clips at 0, giving delta ≈ 0.2. This means delta is actually *anti-correlated* with conflict—the opposite of what I'd expect.

So in the correlational_only ablation, mean(delta[c_hi]) should be less than mean(delta[ctrl]), which would make obs2 negative and cause G2 to fail. Yet it passed with p=0.005, which doesn't add up. The real issue is that my planted worlds are too sensitive to the exact noise and clipping behavior. I need to either construct correlational_only so it genuinely has no contested-specific freeze (making delta_contested ≈ delta_control), or find a cleaner way to lower flip by a constant across all coordinates without clipping artifacts interfering.

Actually, wait — the gate logic itself is fine; it correctly identified correlational_only before with p=0.397. What changed between then and now? Before, both G1 and G2 each consumed n_perm RNG calls for permutation testing, and I don't think I added any RNG calls before them.

Actually, maybe the "passing" run I'm remembering used different parameters. The first validate run used n_perm=1000 (the default in run_validate), and this one should too. Same seed based on hash(kind). So they should produce identical results... unless the hash function itself is behaving differently.

Oh wait — Python's hash() for strings is randomized per process unless PYTHONHASHSEED is set. So `abs(hash(kind)) % (2**31)` gives a different seed each time I run the code, which means the random noise realization changes between runs. For borderline worlds like correlational_only, this can flip whether G2 crosses the significance threshold. That's why the first run passed but this one failed — it's a flaky test caused by non-deterministic seeding. I need to replace the hash-based seed with a fixed deterministic value per kind, and make sure the planted worlds have enough separation that they're robust to noise variations.

Redesigning _plant to be deterministic and stable: I'll use a lookup dict of fixed seeds instead of hashing, and adjust the signal range so flip stays safely within bounds like [0.1, 0.7] to avoid clipping artifacts that could make the ablation effect unpredictable. Rewriting the _plant function... Writing conditional branches... def _plant(kind: str, rng: np.random.Generator, n: int = 4000) -> dict:
    """Build per-arm caps with a known ground truth."""
    W_base_abs = np.abs(rng.normal(0, 1, n))
    W_abs = rng.uniform(0.01, 1.0, n)
    sigma = rng.uniform(0.1, 1.0, n)
    conflict = rng.uniform(-1, 1, n)
    lam_sgd = list(np.linspace(1, 190, 15))          # sharpens toward 2/eta=200
    lam_adam = list(np.linspace(1, 40, 15))          # stays low

    if kind == "void":
        flip = np.zeros(n)
        flip_s = np.zeros(n)
    elif kind == "noise_floor":
        # flip driven by |W| and sigma only; conflict unrelated
        base = 0.6 * (1 - _rank(W_abs) / n) + 0.4 * (_rank(sigma) / n)
        flip = np.clip(base + rng.normal(0, 0.05, n), 0, 1)
        flip_s = np.clip(base + rng.normal(0, 0.05, n), 0, 1)
    elif kind in ("conflict_meter", "correlational_only"):
        base = 0.3 * (1 - _rank(W_abs) / n) + 0.2 * (_rank(sigma) / n)
        signal = 0.6 * (_rank(conflict) / n)
        flip = np.clip(base + signal + rng.normal(0, 0.04, n), 0, 1)
        if kind == "conflict_meter":
            # ablation freezes contested coords (high conflict) strongly
            freeze = 0.6 * (_rank(conflict) / n)
            flip_s = np.clip(flip - freeze + rng.normal(0, 0.03, n), 0, 1)
        else:
            # ablation lowers everything uniformly (no contested-specific freeze)
            flip_s = np.clip(flip - 0.1 + rng.normal(0, 0.03, n), 0, 1)
    else:
        raise ValueError(kind)

    def arm(f):
        return {"flip": f, "conflict": conflict, "W_abs": W_abs,
                "sigma": sigma, "W_base_abs": W_base_abs}

    return {
        "both_sgd": {**arm(flip), "lam_max": lam_sgd},
        "A_sgd": arm(flip_s), "B_sgd": arm(flip_s),
        "both_adam": {**arm(flip), "lam_max": lam_adam},
    }


def run_validate(alpha: float) -> int:
    print("── §P-FLIP-CONFLICT --validate (planted worlds, no model) ──")
    want = {"conflict_meter": "CONFLICT-METER-CONFIRMED",
            "correlational_only": "CORRELATIONAL-ONLY",
            "noise_floor": "NOISE-FLOOR",
            "void": "VOID"}
